</div></div></a></div></div></div></div><div class="readable downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa para"><div class="TopicPara_topicDefinition__nKNQv"><p class="TopicPara_topic_explanation__Sp1wS themeLight TopicPara_topicDefinition__nKNQv undefined" data-testid="topicDefinition"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Exocrine pancreatic insufficiency is caused by decreased synthesis and secretion of digestive enzymes by the pancreas. The most common clinical signs are weight loss and a large volume of loose stools. Dogs are also often polyphagic. Diagnosis is made by measurement of serum trypsin-like immunoreactivity. Treatment includes pancreatic enzyme replacement therapy and supplementation of cobalamin.</span></p></div></div><div class="TopicSubNavigation_ariaAnnouncement__P8fZ1" aria-live="polite"></div><div class="TopicSubNavigation_topicSubNavigation___jimK

class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v50145867" data-testid="topicPara"></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3266878" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Exocrine pancreatic insufficiency (EPI) is a syndrome caused by insufficient synthesis and secretion of digestive enzymes by the exocrine portion of the pancreas. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="797661b6-d8e0-4958-a446-282b45315d17" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">EPI is less common than pancreatitis in both dogs and cats; however, it is the second most common exocrine pancreatic disorder in both species.</span></p><span id="etiology-and-pathophysiology" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor"></span><span id="v3266879" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor"></span><span id="v3266880" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor"></span><section id="Etiology-and-Pathophysiology_v3266879" itemProp="" itemscope="" itemType="" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ" data-testid="topicFHeadSection"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Etiology and Pathophysiology" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Etiology and Pathophysiology of Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency in Dogs and Cats</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3266881" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Pancreatic acinar atrophy is the most common cause of exocrine pancreatic insufficiency in German Shepherd Dogs, "rough" Collies, and Eurasiers (Eurasians), whereas chronic </span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/digestive-system/the-exocrine-pancreas/pancreatitis-in-dogs-and-cats" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">pancreatitis</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> is the most common cause in dogs of other breeds and in cats. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="845836c3-dd3b-4231-ab8e-63b207b88e03" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Rare causes of EPI in dogs and cats are pancreatic and extrapancreatic masses that lead to obstruction of the pancreatic duct. </span></p><section id="v103266778" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSection__knUIS" data-testid="topicGHead"><span></span><div class=""><h3 class="TopicGHead_headerTag__E1d_E readable downloadContent false"><span></span><span class="TopicGHead_topicGHead__avdGK"><span id="bd637a1b-b74d-4f58-abf1-65dc8d7b065e" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSectionWithScroll__rJBcG">Pathogenesis of Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency in Dogs and Cats</span></span></h3></div><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v50145869" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The exocrine pancreas has a remarkable functional reserve, approximately 90% or more of which must be lost before clinical signs of EPI develop. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="39184b72-a698-4d50-8a05-574fc695ed0e" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Pancreatic acinar enzymes play an integral role in the assimilation of all major macronutrients, and a lack of pancreatic digestive enzymes leads primarily to maldigestion. However, animals with EPI also show evidence of </span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/digestive-system/diseases-of-the-small-intestine-in-small-animals/malabsorption-syndromes-in-small-animals" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">malabsorption</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">, the pathogenetic basis of which is less well understood. The nutrients remaining in the intestinal lumen lead to loose, voluminous feces and steatorrhea. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="62b220bc-3db6-4999-87f1-3c78d612fb11" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The lack of absorption of nutrients also causes weight loss and can lead to vitamin deficiencies. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="205b275e-a1ce-4b76-a39f-c5063946c4d9" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">In animals with EPI caused by chronic pancreatitis, destruction of pancreatic tissue may not be limited to the acinar cells, and concurrent </span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/endocrine-system/the-pancreas/diabetes-mellitus-in-dogs-and-cats" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">diabetes mellitus</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> may develop.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="24f01c21-b222-48d8-a730-c348f9e356da" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Cobalamin absorption depends on adequate synthesis and secretion of intrinsic factor. In both dogs and cats, most intrinsic factor is synthesized and secreted by the </span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/digestive-system/the-exocrine-pancreas/overview-of-the-exocrine-pancreas-in-dogs-and-cats" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">exocrine pancreas</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">, and most dogs and almost all cats with EPI are cobalamin deficient. Also, in a study of dogs with EPI, cobalamin deficiency was the only independent risk factor for poor outcome (</span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/digestive-system/the-exocrine-pancreas/exocrine-pancreatic-insufficiency-in-dogs-and-cats#d7a85267-3049-4ef9-bf7e-95cbfe8abd85" aria-label="1 References" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">1</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">). </span></p><div data-testid="pearlDidyouknow" dir="ltr" class="TopicPearlDidYouKnow_pearlDidyouknow__J0GKw undefined veterinary false"><div dir="ltr" class="TopicPearlDidYouKnow_pearlDidyouknowContainer__yJcyK undefined printBreakInside"><div class="TopicPearlDidYouKnow_pearlDidyouknowInner__8XIdn TopicPearlDidYouKnow_PearlOfTheday__NyPLN"><div data-testid="BaseTableView" class="undefined TopicTableView_didYouKnow__QQEqr"><div class="TopicTableView_ariaAnnouncement__prBWM" aria-live="polite"></div><div tabindex="-1"><h3 class="undefined TableHeader_title__REY9f false false
      TableHeader_tableHeaderWithoutScroll__Xnmx_
      undefined" id="99914035-7679-4203-8ef9-dedd9e2d402f"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Pearls & Pitfalls</span></p></h3></div><div tabindex="-1" class="TopicTableView_tableWrap__ZzPqw    "><table class="TopicTableView_tableViewWrapper__NC6RG "><tbody class="undefined"><tr data-testid="TopicTableBodyView"><td data-testid="TopicTableCellView" rowspan="" colSpan="" class="TopicTableCellView_tableData__pQ2zv TopicTableCellView_notinpopup__lcznU "><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            TopicList_tableListView__w4iky undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="6328afea-0fca-4082-99e5-13c93f8af158" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">In both dogs and cats, most intrinsic factor is synthesized and secreted by the exocrine pancreas, and > 80% of dogs and almost all cats with EPI are cobalamin deficient.</span></p></li></ul></td></tr></tbody></table><div class="TopicTableView_mediaprintfooter__cNGyB false"></div></div></div></div></div></div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="06854ab0-f30c-405d-8bb1-65a14e923ead" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Other hypovitaminoses have also been reported in animals with EPI (</span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/digestive-system/the-exocrine-pancreas/exocrine-pancreatic-insufficiency-in-dogs-and-cats#d7a85267-3049-4ef9-bf7e-95cbfe8abd85" aria-label="2 References" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">2</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">).           veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Epidemiology" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Epidemiology of Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency in Dogs and Cats</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="fff44cc7-b51d-425a-afa4-4e464a0e0933" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Exocrine pancreatic insufficiency due to pancreatic acinar atrophy is most frequent in young adult German Shepherd Dogs but has also been described in rough Collies and Eurasiers. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="5faa253f-0f91-428c-8cde-1d9767160b6f" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Dogs with EPI due to other causes and cats can be of any age and breed. </span></p></div></section><span id="clinical-findings" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3266883" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3266884" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="Clinical-Findings_v3266883" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Clinical Findings" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Clinical Findings of Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency in Dogs and Cats</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="7cecd07e-af2d-4a45-b5b4-161e6a19fad7" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Clinical signs of EPI most commonly reported are weight loss and loose stools; in addition, dogs also commonly have polyphagia. </span></b></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="e2e441ff-bd00-4122-9269-23ac934a5dd6" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Vomiting and anorexia are observed in some patients and may be a sign of concurrent conditions rather than of EPI itself. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="858c78dc-f6c2-48ce-b898-10ed0e281634" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Feces are most commonly pale, loose, and voluminous and may be malodorous. In rare cases, watery diarrhea can be observed. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="c56045bf-6edf-4a24-820d-7152e12c10b3" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">In a small portion of cats with EPI, the high fat content of the feces can lead to a greasy appearance of the coat, especially in the perianal and tail region.</span></p></div></section><span id="diagnosis" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3266886" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3266887" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="Diagnosis_v3266886" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Diagnosis" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Diagnosis of Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency in Dogs and Cats</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v50145874" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Measurement of serum trypsin-like immunoreactivity</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="870aa1dd-089e-49e5-bec4-87b6f62f9055" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Measurement of serum cobalamin and folate concentrations</span></p></li></ul><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3266888" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">A low serum trypsin-like immunoreactivity (TLI) concentration (≤ 5.5 mcg/L in dogs or ≤ 8.0 mcg/L in cats) is diagnostic for exocrine pancreatic insufficiency. </span></b></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="ba364c0f-624e-460b-a4b5-1e0d187257ca" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Because digestion of a macronutrient can often be accomplished by more than one enzyme, lack of exocrine pancreatic secretions does not necessarily lead to clinical signs. For example, subclinical EPI has been reported in several German Shepherd Dogs. These dogs had severely decreased serum TLI concentrations and a lack of exocrine pancreatic tissue but no or only intermittent clinical signs of EPI.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3266889" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">An assay that measures fecal elastase in dogs has been validated. Unfortunately, some healthy dogs or dogs with chronic small intestinal disease may have a severely decreased fecal elastase concentration, making this test much less reliable than serum TLI concentration.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="8a90dfe9-fa2b-4859-a09c-8a7a397d91ff" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Serum cobalamin and folate concentrations should be routinely evaluated in small animals with suspected EPI.</span></b></p></div></section><span id="treatment" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor"></span><span id="v3266890" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor"></span><span id="v3266891" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor"></span><section id="Treatment_v3266890" itemProp="" itemscope="" itemType="" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ" data-testid="topicFHeadSection"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Treatment" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Treatment of Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency in Dogs and Cats</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v50145877" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Pancreatic enzyme replacement therapy (PERT)</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="586e40a6-c8a8-4cba-ad35-ef7e07e26082" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Cobalamin supplementation</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="e23a6455-0028-4693-9b67-e9c7e6f24d90" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Dietary change</span></p></li></ul><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3266892" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Most dogs and cats with exocrine pancreatic insufficiency can be successfully managed by </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">PERT</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="5d015275-3e44-4939-93ef-242d930f2b38" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Various enzyme formulations for PERT are available. Powders are more effective than tablets, capsules, and especially enteric-coated products. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="d25f8fd8-2114-4384-b872-d7e051a81c01" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Initially, for dogs, 1 teaspoon/10 kg body weight should be given in food with each meal, and for cats, 1 teaspoon/cat in food with each meal. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3266893" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Fresh pancreatic tissue may be a viable alternative to the use of powder; 30–90 g (1–3 ounces) of raw chopped pancreas can replace 1 teaspoon of pancreatic extract. Raw pancreas can be kept frozen for several months without loss of enzymatic activity. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="ede96723-c4aa-4095-9b49-4aa9b7439c76" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Preincubation of the food with pancreatic enzymes or supplementation with bile salts is not necessary.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="6503070c-3bf4-4f0b-afdb-4ce897aa2d30" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Once clinical signs have completely resolved, the dose can be slowly decreased until the lowest effective dose has been reached. However, the lowest effective dose can vary between enzyme batches. In one study, oral bleeding was reported in 3 of 25 dogs with EPI treated with pancreatic enzyme supplements; bleeding stopped in all three dogs after the dose was decreased (</span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/digestive-system/the-exocrine-pancreas/exocrine-pancreatic-insufficiency-in-dogs-and-cats#d7a85267-3049-4ef9-bf7e-95cbfe8abd85" aria-label="4 References" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">4</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">). Moistening the food and pancreatic powder mix may also decrease the frequency of this adverse effect.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3266894" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Even though PERT decreases clinical signs in almost all animals, nutrient absorption, especially that of fats, is not normalized. Feeding low-fat diets to accommodate impaired fat digestion has been suggested; however, this may further decrease fat assimilation and lead to deficiencies of fat-soluble vitamins and/or essential fatty acids and is thus not recommended. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="04cfae93-7e6d-4540-9a44-82a2a89afa88" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Some types of dietary fiber interfere with pancreatic enzyme activity, and a </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">diet low in insoluble or nonfermentable fiber</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> should be fed. In contrast, fermentable fiber (eg, fructooligosaccharides, beet pulp, or similar) can be given.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3266895" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Enzyme supplementation alone may not lead to complete resolution of clinical signs; cobalamin deficiency should be considered as a possible cause. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="8688579a-c5eb-4fb8-9bd9-e1e56c13b881" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Dogs and cats with cobalamin deficiency, suggested by a severely decreased serum cobalamin concentration, should be </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">supplemented with cobalamin</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">, either parenterally (dogs: 25 mcg/kg, SC, once weekly; cats: 250 mcg/cat, SC, once weekly) or orally (dogs: 25 mcg/kg, PO, every 24 hours; cats: 250 mcg/cat, PO, every 24 hours). </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3266896" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Some animals may not respond to enzyme supplementation and cobalamin therapy and likely have concurrent small intestinal disease. Animals with EPI commonly have concurrent small intestinal dysbiosis and may benefit from </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">antimicrobial therapy</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> (eg, tylosin) or </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">fecal microbiota transplantation</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> (FMT). </span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/digestive-system/diseases-of-the-small-intestine-in-small-animals/chronic-enteropathies-in-small-animals" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Chronic inflammatory enteropathy</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> (often referred to as inflammatory bowel disease) also occurs in some animals with EPI. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="e2fbbeb7-4f7b-43b1-a7ec-b92c916f4a7a" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Although antacid therapy is unnecessary in most cases of EPI, in patients that do not respond to PERT, administration of a proton pump inhibitor (eg, omeprazole, 0.7–1.0 mg/kg, every 12 hours) can be tried to prevent inactivation of pancreatic enzymes during transit to the small intestine.</span></p></div></section><span id="prognosis" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3266897" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v3266898" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="Prognosis_v3266897" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Prognosis" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Prognosis of Exocrine Pancreatic Insufficiency in Dogs and Cats</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3266899" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Exocrine pancreatic insufficiency results from an irreversible loss of pancreatic acinar tissue in most cases, and recovery is rare. However, with appropriate management and monitoring, affected animals usually gain weight quickly, pass normal feces, and can live a normal life for a normal lifespan.</span></p></div></section><span id="key-points" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v50145880" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v50145881" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="Key-Points_v50145880" class="TopicFHead_showKeyPoints__RB4kR veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 id="KeyPoints_v50145881" class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Key Points" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Key Points</span><span class="TopicFHead_keypoints__9WCzn"></span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v50145884" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Exocrine pancreatic insufficiency is diagnosed by measurement of serum trypsin-like immunoreactivity concentration.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v50145886" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Treatment of dogs and cats with EPI includes pancreatic enzyme replacement therapy and cobalamin supplementation.</span></p></li></ul></div></section><span id="for-more-information" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v50145887" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v50145888" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="For-More-Information_v50145887" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 id="Further_v50145888" class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="For More Information" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>For More Information</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v50145891" data-testid="topicPara"><span><a class="TopicLink_topicLink__qJmYk veterinary undefined" href="https://vetmed.tamu.edu/gilab/service/assays/tli/" target="" rel="" data-testid="topicLink">Serum trypsin-like immunoreactivity (TLI)</a></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">. Texas A&M University Gastrointestinal Laboratory.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v50145894" data-testid="topicPara"><span><a class="TopicLink_topicLink__qJmYk veterinary undefined" href="https://vetmed.tamu.edu/gilab/service/assays/b12folate/" target="" rel="" data-testid="topicLink">Serum cobalamin (vitamin B12 and folate)</a></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">. Texas A&M University Gastrointestinal Laboratory.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v50145897" data-testid="topicPara"><span><a class="TopicLink_topicLink__qJmYk veterinary undefined" href="https://epi4dogs.com/" target="" rel="" data-testid="topicLink">EPI for Dogs Foundation</a></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v50145900" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Also see pet owner content regarding disorders of the pancreas in </span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/cat-owners/digestive-disorders-of-cats/disorders-of-the-pancreas-in-cats" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">cats</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> and </span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/dog-owners/digestive-disorders-of-dogs/pancreatitis-and-other-disorders-of-the-pancreas-in-dogs" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">dogs</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">.</span></p></li></ul></div></section><span id="references" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v103266819" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="d7a85267-3049-4ef9-bf7e-95cbfe8abd85" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="References_v103266819" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
